# Day 1, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.1.0.body]
Welcome to Pixel IT Partners. Alan and Garrison own the place and I run the floor for them. Tickets come in on ConnectWhy PSA, you fix things with LabRat RMM, and you only close a ticket once it is really fixed. Reopened tickets make me sad. Clients also call and text, so check your phone (P) now and then. - Morgan

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: from
[Story.DAYS.2.0.from]
Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.2.0.subject]
Daily chore: verify backups

# Day 2, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.2.0.body]
Every morning open LabRat RMM, click BACKUPS and check each client. Backup jobs can fail silently and nobody notices until the day it really matters. It takes thirty seconds, until your security skills are good enough for the backup watch to do it for you. - Morgan

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: from
[Story.DAYS.4.0.from]
Morgan Reyes - Manager

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: subject
[Story.DAYS.4.0.subject]
Windows XP is going away

# Day 4, 9:05, MAIL: body
[Story.DAYS.4.0.body]
Microsoft ends support for Windows XP and Office 2003 on April 8. After that there are no more security patches, so every XP machine at every client is a liability. Dr. Patel at Acme Dental is first. Survey the old PC (old software, old drivers, odd settings), back it up before you move anything, migrate, then verify with the user before you wipe it. - Morgan

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > title

RANSOMWARE REVIEW

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > intro
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.intro]
{me}, got half an hour? Before the ransomware week turns into a story we tell badly, let us write down what happened: the timeline, the real cause, what we change. It is blameless, we look at the process and not at a person. Or we can skip it.

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > go
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.go]
SIT DOWN WITH MORGAN

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > skip
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.skip]
SKIP IT, TOO BUSY

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > skipped
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.skipped]
Morgan nods and puts the marker back. 'We will see how it goes,' she says. It sounds like she thinks it will happen again.

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > cause_q
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.cause_q]
Morgan draws a timeline on the whiteboard. The files were encrypted at 10:30. The backup alert was in the queue for two days. What was the root cause?

# Story > POSTMORTEMS > ransom > causes > 0 > label
[Story.POSTMORTEMS.ransom.causes.0.label]

# Nerds > THREADS > 0 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.0.title]
Print spooler keeps dying on 2008 R2

# Nerds > THREADS > 0 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.0.posts.0.text]
The spooler stops every morning around nine. Restart it and it works until tomorrow. Server is 2008 R2, about 40 printers.

# Nerds > THREADS > 0 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.0.posts.1.text]
Delete the stuck job files in the spool folder, then check for the one bad driver. It is always the 32 bit driver from 2009.

# Nerds > THREADS > 0 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.0.posts.2.text]
Seconded. Twenty seven of our forty printers were running one cheap driver. Replaced it and no more morning deaths.

# Nerds > THREADS > 0 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.0.answer]
Clear the spool folder, stop and start the service, then remove the old 32 bit driver. Mine was a copier from 2009.

# Nerds > THREADS > 1 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.1.title]
Users locked out after bad passwords: GPO?

# Nerds > THREADS > 1 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.1.posts.0.text]
Whole office locked out on Monday mornings. Is the lockout policy too tight? It says 3 tries.

# Nerds > THREADS > 1 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.1.posts.1.text]
3 tries is a policy written by someone who has never met a user. Set 10, reset after 30 minutes. And look for the phone that still has the old password saved.

# Nerds > THREADS > 1 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.1.posts.2.text]
The phone. It is always the phone. Check the mail sync on the boss's phone first.

# Nerds > THREADS > 1 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.1.answer]
Raise the threshold to 10 and the reset to 30 minutes, then hunt the phone or laptop with the old saved password.

# Nerds > THREADS > 2 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.2.title]
Disk full, where is all the temp junk?

# Nerds > THREADS > 2 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.2.posts.0.text]
C: is at 98 percent on a file server. Not much installed. Where does the space go?

# Nerds > THREADS > 2 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.2.posts.1.text]
Run a folder size tool from the root. Check the Windows folder for old installer files, and look for a log that never rotates.

# Nerds > THREADS > 2 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.2.posts.2.text]
In my case it was 40 GB of IIS logs. Nobody had set the cleanup. Twelve months of them.

# Nerds > THREADS > 2 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.2.answer]
Scan the drive by folder size. Usually it is logs, old profiles or the installer cache. Clear those, then set a cleanup job.

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.3.title]
Blue screen 0x7B after a controller swap

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.3.posts.0.text]
Swapped the RAID card on an old server, now it blue screens 0x7B at boot. Disks are fine on the new card.

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.3.posts.1.text]
0x7B is inaccessible boot device. The new controller driver is not in the image. Boot a repair disk and load the driver, or switch it to the old mode in the BIOS.

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.3.posts.2.text]
just reinstall windows lol

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > posts > 3 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.3.posts.3.text]
Nobody reinstall anything. Read the post above.

# Nerds > THREADS > 3 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.3.answer]
Boot the repair disk, load the new controller driver, then reboot. Do not reinstall, the data is fine.

# Nerds > THREADS > 4 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.4.title]
CryptoLocker: shadow copies, any luck?

# Nerds > THREADS > 4 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.4.posts.0.text]
A client got hit. Files all renamed and a ransom note, 300 dollars in bitcoin. They have no backup. Is paying the only way?

# Nerds > THREADS > 4 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.4.posts.1.text]
Try shadow copies first. The thing does not always remove them. Right click the folder, previous versions. Disconnect the machine from the network before you do anything else.

# Nerds > THREADS > 4 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.4.posts.2.text]
We got about 40 percent back that way. Pay only if you must, and tell the client it may not work. Then sell them a backup.

# Nerds > THREADS > 4 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.4.answer]
Unplug it from the network, check previous versions and shadow copies, and restore what you can. Paying is a last resort.

# Nerds > THREADS > 5 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.5.title]
Robocopy /MIR and NTFS permissions

# Nerds > THREADS > 5 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.5.posts.0.text]
Mirrored a share to a new server with robocopy /MIR. Files are there but nobody can open half of them.

# Nerds > THREADS > 5 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.5.posts.1.text]
You need /COPY:DATSOU, or /SEC. Default does not copy the security. And careful, /MIR deletes at the destination.

# Nerds > THREADS > 5 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.5.posts.2.text]
I have seen /MIR delete a whole department. Run it with /L first. Always /L first.

# Nerds > THREADS > 5 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.5.answer]
Use /COPYALL or /COPY:DATSOU, run with /L first to preview, and only then run it for real. /MIR deletes extras.

# Nerds > THREADS > 6 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.6.title]
VLANs for voice and data on a cheap switch

# Nerds > THREADS > 6 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.6.posts.0.text]
Office wants phones and PCs on separate VLANs on one cheap managed switch. Phones plug in, PCs plug into the phone.

# Nerds > THREADS > 6 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.6.posts.1.text]
Tag the voice VLAN on the phone port, leave data untagged as the native VLAN. The phone passes the PC through.

# Nerds > THREADS > 6 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.6.posts.2.text]
Check the switch supports it on the same port. Some cheap ones only do one tagged VLAN per access port.

# Nerds > THREADS > 6 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.6.answer]
Make each port a trunk with data untagged and voice tagged, so the phone tags itself and passes the PC through.

# Nerds > THREADS > 7 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.7.title]
Stopping RDP brute force: what do you use?

# Nerds > THREADS > 7 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.7.posts.0.text]
Event log full of failed logins from China. Port 3389 is forwarded to the server. Please tell me what to do.

# Nerds > THREADS > 7 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.7.posts.1.text]
First, stop forwarding 3389. Use a VPN. If you cannot, change the port and use a firewall that blocks repeat failures.

# Nerds > THREADS > 7 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.7.posts.2.text]
Brother, I have seen this sin many times. Close the door. Bless you.

# Nerds > THREADS > 7 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.7.answer]
Stop forwarding 3389. Put it behind a VPN or at least a firewall that blocks repeat failed logins, and check for weak passwords.

# Nerds > THREADS > 9 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.9.title]
Script to clean temp files on every client

# Nerds > THREADS > 9 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.9.posts.0.text]
Need a script to clear temp folders on all our clients every week. Anything safe to run unattended?

# Nerds > THREADS > 9 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.9.posts.1.text]
Delete files older than a week in the user temp and the Windows temp. Skip anything locked. Run it as a script in the RMM, not by hand.

# Nerds > THREADS > 9 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.9.posts.2.text]
format c: fixes everything

# Nerds > THREADS > 9 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.9.answer]
Delete files older than seven days from the Windows and user temp folders, skip the locked ones, and run it weekly as a scheduled script.

# Nerds > THREADS > 12 > title

Patch Tuesday auto-approve rules

# Nerds > THREADS > 12 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.12.posts.0.text]
Do you auto-approve everything on Patch Tuesday or wait? Last month a patch broke printing for a whole client.

# Nerds > THREADS > 12 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.12.posts.1.text]
Critical and security on a test group first, the rest a week later. Never on the same day as month end.

# Nerds > THREADS > 12 > posts > 2 > text

Patch Tuesday is not the day to find out. Wait a week.

# Nerds > THREADS > 12 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.12.answer]
Approve critical updates to a small test group first, wait a few days, then roll out. Never patch the whole fleet on the first day.

# Nerds > THREADS > 13 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.13.title]
Which backup alerts are noise?

# Nerds > THREADS > 13 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.13.posts.0.text]
I get 200 backup emails a day. Which ones do you really act on?

# Nerds > THREADS > 13 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.13.posts.1.text]
Failed twice in a row is a call. Missing for 24 hours is a call. Success mails go to the bin. Warnings about open files, ignore.

# Nerds > THREADS > 13 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.13.posts.2.text]
A green tick is a rumour. Test a restore once a month.

# Nerds > THREADS > 13 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.13.answer]
Act on two failures in a row or a missing job, ignore success mails and open file warnings, and test one restore every month.

# Nerds > THREADS > 21 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.21.title]
Custom tab that shows the warranty date

# Nerds > THREADS > 21 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.21.posts.0.text]
Wanted a tab on each computer showing the warranty end. Anyone done this with the serial number?

# Nerds > THREADS > 21 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.21.posts.1.text]
Use a query on the BIOS serial and the vendor's page. Cache it in your own table or they will block you.

# Nerds > THREADS > 21 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.21.posts.2.text]
I do that for two vendors. Third one has a captcha. It wins.

# Nerds > THREADS > 21 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.21.answer]
Read the BIOS serial, look up the warranty on the vendor site, cache the answer in your own table and show it on the tab.

# Nerds > THREADS > 22 > title

# Nerds > THREADS > 22 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.22.posts.1.text]
Each session name equals the machine name. Build the link from the name and open it in a new window.

# Nerds > THREADS > 22 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.22.posts.2.text]

[Nerds.THREADS.22.answer]

# Nerds > THREADS > 42 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.42.posts.1.text]
Install the vendor agent so it exposes SNMP, then walk the tree and look for the logical disk status. Anything not 'ok' is an alert.

# Nerds > THREADS > 42 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.42.posts.2.text]
Walk it twice, once with a disk pulled. Then you know which number changes.

# Nerds > THREADS > 42 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.42.answer]
Install the controller's management agent so it exposes SNMP, then walk the tree and alert on any logical disk that is not ok.

# Nerds > THREADS > 43 > title

# Nerds > THREADS > 57 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.57.title]
How to post a script (read this first)

# Nerds > THREADS > 57 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.57.posts.0.text]
Title says what it does. Say what it was tested on. Put the script in code tags. Say how to undo it. Scripts with no undo get deleted. Not affiliated with the software vendor. Test on a lab system.

# Nerds > THREADS > 57 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.57.posts.1.text]
And comment your code. In a year you will be the stranger who needs help.

# Nerds > THREADS > 57 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.57.posts.2.text]
I POSTED MY SCRIPT AND IT HAS NO REPLIES WHY

# Nerds > THREADS > 58 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.58.title]
Detect files with double extensions (invoice.pdf.exe)

# Nerds > THREADS > 58 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.58.posts.0.text]
Looking for a script that finds files like invoice.pdf.exe on the file shares.

# Nerds > THREADS > 58 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.58.posts.1.text]
Match names with a document extension followed by an executable one. Include .scr and .com. Log the paths, do not delete them.

# Nerds > THREADS > 58 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.58.posts.2.text]
Also check if the user's explorer hides known extensions. It does. That is why it works.

# Nerds > THREADS > 58 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.58.answer]
Search the shares for a document extension followed by exe, scr or com, log the paths, and turn on showing file extensions by policy.

# Nerds > THREADS > 59 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.59.title]
Restart a stuck service remotely

# Nerds > THREADS > 59 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.59.posts.0.text]
A service hangs on stopping every few days. Need a script that kills it and starts it again.

# Nerds > THREADS > 59 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.59.posts.1.text]
Get the process ID of the service, stop the process if the stop times out, then start the service. Log it so you can prove it happened.

# Nerds > THREADS > 59 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.59.posts.2.text]
just reboot the whole server

# Nerds > THREADS > 59 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.59.answer]
Stop the service with a timeout, kill the process if it hangs, start it again and write a line to the log.

# Nerds > THREADS > 60 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.60.title]
Find users with passwords that never expire

# Nerds > THREADS > 60 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.60.posts.0.text]
Audit time. I need a list of accounts with the password never expires flag.

# Nerds > THREADS > 60 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.60.posts.1.text]
Query the directory for the flag, sort by last password change, export a CSV. Exclude service accounts you have documented.

# Nerds > THREADS > 60 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.60.posts.2.text]
The CSV goes to the owner. The owner says 'oh'.

# Nerds > THREADS > 60 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.60.answer]
Query the directory for the never expires flag, sort by last password change, export it to a CSV and review the service accounts.

# Nerds > THREADS > 61 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.61.title]
Script: list installed software to a CSV

# Nerds > THREADS > 61 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.61.posts.0.text]
Want an inventory of installed software for all machines at a client, in one CSV.

# Nerds > THREADS > 61 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.61.posts.1.text]
Read the uninstall keys in the registry, both the 32 bit and 64 bit locations. Add the machine name and run it from the RMM.

# Nerds > THREADS > 61 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.61.posts.2.text]
Do not use the slow WMI product class. It also triggers a repair on every MSI package.

# Nerds > THREADS > 61 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.61.answer]
Read the uninstall keys for both the 32 and 64 bit software, add the machine name, and export one CSV per client.

# Nerds > THREADS > 69 > title
[Nerds.THREADS.69.title]
Writing a plugin that reads SNMP

# Nerds > THREADS > 69 > posts > 0 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.69.posts.0.text]
Want a plugin that reads SNMP from switches and shows port speeds in a tab.

# Nerds > THREADS > 69 > posts > 1 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.69.posts.1.text]
Use a library, do not write the protocol yourself. Poll slowly. Cache the results.

# Nerds > THREADS > 69 > posts > 2 > text
[Nerds.THREADS.69.posts.2.text]
And walk the tree on a lab switch first. Real switches have opinions.

# Nerds > THREADS > 69 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.69.answer]
Use an SNMP library, poll slowly, cache the results in your own table and test on a lab switch before production.

# Nerds > THREADS > 70 > title

# Nerds > THREADS > 70 > posts > 1 > text

# Nerds > THREADS > 70 > posts > 2 > text

The lab is not optional. The lab is the thing.

# Nerds > THREADS > 70 > answer
[Nerds.THREADS.70.answer]
Build a small lab with a spare box and a free hypervisor and restore a copy of the database before testing.

# ======================= Hints > DAY1 =======================
# Hints > DAY1 > 0 > text
[Hints.DAY1.0.text]
ARROWS OR A/D MOVE. HOLD SHIFT TO RUN. SPACE JUMPS.

# Hints > DAY1 > 1 > text
[Hints.DAY1.1.text]
E USES WHAT IS NEAR: DOORS, THE COFFEE MACHINE, CHAIRS. TAB PICKS THE TARGET.

# Hints > DAY1 > 2 > text
[Hints.DAY1.2.text]
SIT AT A DESK AND PRESS E ON THE MONITOR TO OPEN THE COMPUTER. ESC CLOSES IT.

# Hints > DAY1 > 3 > text
[Hints.DAY1.3.text]
P OPENS YOUR PHONE: MAIL, TICKETS AND TEXTS. Q WAVES, C ASKS A COWORKER TO FOLLOW.

# Hints > DAY1 > 4 > text
[Hints.DAY1.4.text]
TICKETS APPEAR IN THE PSA. FIX THEM IN THE RMM, THEN CLOSE THEM IN THE PSA.

# Hints > DAY1 > 5 > text
[Hints.DAY1.5.text]
SEATED? HOLD SHIFT AND PRESS A OR D TO ROLL YOUR CHAIR. E OR ANY MOVE KEY STANDS YOU UP.

# Hints > DAY1 > 6 > text
[Hints.DAY1.6.text]
M MUTES OR RESTORES THE SOUND.


# ======================= Hints > MENTOR =======================
# Hints > MENTOR > pc
[Hints.MENTOR.pc]
DAN: SIT AT YOUR DESK AND PRESS E ON THE MONITOR. THAT OPENS THE COMPUTER.

# Hints > MENTOR > wait
[Hints.MENTOR.wait]
DAN: A TICKET WILL ARRIVE SOON. IT SHOWS UP IN THE PSA ON YOUR COMPUTER.

# Hints > MENTOR > take
[Hints.MENTOR.take]
DAN: OPEN THE PSA AND TAKE YOUR FIRST TICKET.

# Hints > MENTOR > close
[Hints.MENTOR.close]
DAN: THE FIX IS DONE. CLOSE THE TICKET IN THE PSA.

# Hints > MENTOR > done
[Hints.MENTOR.done]
DAN: THAT IS ONE CLOSED. YOU ARE ON YOUR WAY.

# ======================= Expo > TEXT =======================
# Expo > TEXT > invite_subject
[Expo.TEXT.invite_subject]
Rodent Nation 2014: top performers go

# Expo > TEXT > invite_body
[Expo.TEXT.invite_body]
LabRat's yearly user conference, RODENT NATION, is in Orlando this June. The company pays for one tech: whoever hits the KPIs by day 16. Reputation 55 or more, SLA misses under 20 percent of closes, reopens under 15 percent of closes, and PowerShell level 2. Hit three of the four and you go. - Morgan

# Expo > TEXT > progress
[Expo.TEXT.progress]
KPI check before the Rodent Nation trip: %s. Three of four wins the ticket.

# Expo > TEXT > yes_subject
[Expo.TEXT.yes_subject]
Pack your bags: Rodent Nation

# Expo > TEXT > yes_body
[Expo.TEXT.yes_body]
You hit the KPIs (%s). You fly out tomorrow for a day of sessions: labs, panels, the vendor hall and the keynote. Dan will cover your queue. Come back smarter. - Morgan

# Expo > TEXT > no_subject
[Expo.TEXT.no_subject]
Rodent Nation: not this year

# Expo > TEXT > no_body
[Expo.TEXT.no_body]
You did not quite make the KPIs (%s), so Dan is going to Rodent Nation this year. He will bring back notes. Aim for it next year. - Morgan

# Expo > TEXT > arrive
[Expo.TEXT.arrive]
RODENT NATION 2014. Orlando, a resort the size of a small town: palm trees in the lobby, carpet with swirls, a ballroom that fits a thousand MSP nerds, and breakout rooms down every hallway. Your badge says TECH and has a little rat on it.

# Expo > TEXT > keynote
[Expo.TEXT.keynote]
The LabRat CEO takes the stage in the ballroom under a sign that says AUTOMATE EVERYTHING. Eleven hundred people applaud a slide about a dashboard. Meanwhile your phone buzzes: the queue back home is not empty.

# Expo > TEXT > keynote_listen
[Expo.TEXT.keynote_listen]
You put the phone away and listen. The CEO says the future is scripts, monitoring and fewer truck rolls. You take notes. (+3 customer XP)

# Expo > TEXT > keynote_peek
[Expo.TEXT.keynote_peek]
You check the queue under the table. Dan has it, but you leave a few notes. It costs you half the keynote.

# Expo > TEXT > hall
[Expo.TEXT.hall]
The solutions pavilion: a long row of booths, free pens, a man in a rat costume, and every vendor wants a scan of your badge.

# Expo > TEXT > hall_swag
[Expo.TEXT.hall_swag]
You collect a bag of pens, stress balls and a USB drive nobody will ever plug in. Alan will want a pen. (coworkers like you a little more)

# Expo > TEXT > hall_nerds
[Expo.TEXT.hall_nerds]
You find the LabRat Nerds forum regulars at the community table. Real faces behind the nicknames. (+4 forum karma)

# Expo > TEXT > hall_vendor
[Expo.TEXT.hall_vendor]
A backup vendor walks you through a disaster recovery demo that actually works. You take the brochure. (+6 security XP)

# Expo > TEXT > raffle_win
[Expo.TEXT.raffle_win]
They call your badge number: you won the Rock Star Upgrade! A new laptop bag, a hotel suite night and a dinner for two. You sleep like a baby. (energy full, +3 reputation)

# Expo > TEXT > raffle_lose
[Expo.TEXT.raffle_lose]
Not your number. A man from Tulsa wins the Rock Star Upgrade and cries. Oh well.

# Expo > TEXT > wrap
[Expo.TEXT.wrap]
The last session lets out. You fly home with a tote bag, a head full of ideas and a lanyard you will keep. Tomorrow the queue will want to hear about it.

# Expo > TEXT > dan_back
[Expo.TEXT.dan_back]
Dan is back from Rodent Nation with a tote bag and one thing he swears will change your life: always label the patch panel. (+6 networking XP)

# Expo > TEXT > home_pile
[Expo.TEXT.home_pile]
While you were away tickets piled up. Dan did what he could.

# Expo > TEXT > home_ok
[Expo.TEXT.home_ok]
You checked in during the keynote, so the pile is small. Dan says thanks.

# Expo > TEXT > report
[Expo.TEXT.report]
RODENT NATION TRIP

# Expo > TEXT > checkin
[Expo.TEXT.checkin]
Welcome to Rodent Nation! Here is your badge, a lanyard, the agenda and a tote bag full of flyers. The ballroom opens at nine. The vendor hall is open all day.

You look it over. Nothing wrong with this one.

# Onsite > TEXT > inspected
[Onsite.TEXT.inspected]
You already looked at that one.

# Onsite > TEXT > found_ticket
[Onsite.TEXT.found_ticket]
You log a ticket. The team will pick it up. The client likes that you spotted it.

# Onsite > TEXT > found_project
[Onsite.TEXT.found_project]
You note it as an opportunity. A quote will be waiting for you in the morning.

# Onsite > TEXT > found_note
[Onsite.TEXT.found_note]
You make a mental note. It might become a problem.

# Onsite > TEXT > found_nocampaign
[Onsite.TEXT.found_nocampaign]
You would log a ticket for that.

# Onsite > TEXT > missed_mail
[Onsite.TEXT.missed_mail]

# RivalTouches > TIPS > Alan
[RivalTouches.TIPS.Alan]
A backup on a USB drive in a freezer bag in the trunk of a car is not a backup. It is a hope. Restore one file somewhere else, then you know.

# RivalTouches > TIPS > Dan
[RivalTouches.TIPS.Dan]
Never trust the little green light on the backup box. Restore one file from every client once a quarter and write down the date.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.title]
THE FREEZER BAG

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.title]
FIND IT

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.text]
The whole backup is one USB drive in a freezer bag. What do you do with it first?

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.choices.0.label]
PHOTOGRAPH THE BAG AND THE LIGHT

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.choices.0.note]
Photographed. That is evidence.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.choices.1.label]
UNPLUG IT AND LOOK

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 1
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.0.choices.1.note]
A drive with a label from 2011.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.title]
TEST THE RESTORE

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.text]
Does anything come back off that drive? There is a spare PC in the corner.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.choices.0.label]
RESTORE TO THE SPARE PC

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.choices.0.note]
The restore failed halfway. Now you know.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.choices.1.label]
TRUST THE LIGHT

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 2
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.1.choices.1.note]
The light is on. That is all you know.

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.2.title]
PUT IT RIGHT

# job: THE FREEZER BAG, step 3
[Playbook.BOOKS.tk_backup.steps.2.text]
What replaces the freezer bag?
